# GRPC migration

## gRPC gzip/deflate response compatibility — 2026-10-17

Compressed reflection follow-up — 2026-10-17: the maintained reflection-arrival HTTP2 peer can now encode its existing complete response frames with gzip or zlib-deflate and asserts the production client advertises the selected encoding. Production reflect/Driver::open reads compressed service lists and descriptor replies, reconstructs the same pinned dependency order, then parses the pinned LegacyReflection body to exact expected protobuf wire. Existing uncompressed sequential control passes too. No alternate discovery implementation or new runner was introduced.

Exact cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked --lib grpc_transport::arrival_tests::reflection_arrival_sequential_ passes2tests/91filtered/runtime0.02s/compile34s. One new test loops only gzip/deflate; one retained test validates the helper's identity path. Five existing eventsource-stream warnings; rustfmt applied. No additional product change, native build, full reflection matrix, filesystem/profile/clipboard mutation or hosted provider. This closes bounded v1 compressed-reflection wire follow-up for f39d726b; v1alpha compression, exact decompressed limits, failure/cancellation/WebView/provider/platform and full parity remain open.



Enabled the existing Tonic0.14.6 deflate feature and accept_compressed(Deflate) alongside gzip for application calls. Reflection Driver::open now accepts both gzip and deflate for v1/v1alpha sessions using the same configured client. Existing decoded limits remain20MiB for messages/8MiB for schema, and outbound messages keep their existing identity encoding. Cargo.lock/package versions are unchanged; flate2 was already present. Archive package manifest declares @grpc/grpc-js^1.14.4; published1.14.4 compression-filter.js was read without execution through Bun fetch and confirms identity/deflate/gzip advertisement and zlib inflate. Official Tonic feature API: https://docs.rs/tonic/0.14.6/tonic/codec/enum.CompressionEncoding.html . Published source: https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@grpc/grpc-js@1.14.4/build/src/compression-filter.js (web opener unavailable, direct read succeeded).

Extended the same owned HTTP2 call regression with actual zlib-wrapped deflate and gzip protobuf frames carrying value=owned. Peer verifies advertised algorithms; real production call emits the exact decoded field, expected status/details and duplicate metadata. Prior identity unary/trailers-only OK/error shapes retain coverage: five shapes in one exact test. cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked --lib grpc_transport::status_tests::call_preserves_status_messages_over_http2 -- --exact passes1test/91filtered/runtime0.02s/compile41s. cargo check --locked passes (~10s), rustfmt applied; five existing eventsource-stream warnings. No new test files/scripts/native release build or clipboard/profile changes. Actual compressed reflection wire, other RPC/JSON modes, decompressed-limit/cancellation/provider/WebView/platform and complete gRPC parity remain open; current test uses ProtoJSON unary compressed responses.



## Successful status details — 2026-10-17

HTTP2 transport follow-up (parentef690065): one maintained Rust regression in the existing status_tests module now invokes the real grpc_transport::call against owned loopback HTTP2 peers, reusing the existing Hyper/Tonic test pattern. Three bounded shapes pass: unary empty-protobuf response plus final OK trailers; zero-message server-streaming trailers-only OK; immediate trailers-only PermissionDenied. Each peer verifies exact request method path and framed empty protobuf input. Terminal events retain the exact percent-decoded Thai/plus/percent message, expected code and separate duplicate metadata rows; only the unary case emits one message. Owned peers are aborted/joined before assertions on the result. No external service, OS clipboard or profile mutation.

Exact cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked --lib grpc_transport::status_tests::call_preserves_status_messages_over_http2 -- --exact passes1test/91filtered/runtime0.01s/compile28s. No production code change this follow-up, no new files/runners/release build or replay of the full suite. Five existing eventsource-stream warnings remain. This supplies bounded real HTTP2 call/trailers-only evidence for the prior fix; WebView/IPC/history persistence, compression/deadline/cancellation/provider/platform and complete gRPC parity remain open.



Successful calls previously hardcoded an empty details string, discarding grpc-message. grpc_transport.rs now percent-decodes the successful terminal metadata using the existing percent-encoding dependency and retains metadata_rows unchanged. Tonic0.14.6 stores successful trailers-only status in initial response metadata with an empty body; that block is retained as the terminal fallback. Normal final trailers take precedence. Unary response cardinality and non-OK status handling are unchanged. Official contract https://github.com/grpc/grpc/blob/master/doc/PROTOCOL-HTTP2.md permits Status-Message in trailers even with OK; installed Tonic client/codec/status sources confirm both metadata paths.

Exact cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked --lib grpc_transport::status_tests::successful_status_preserves_encoded_message_and_metadata -- --exact passes1test/90filtered (runtime0s, compile27s). Public Tonic Status.add_header constructs percent-encoded Unicode/plus/percent and duplicate/binary metadata; serialization verifies preserved details, separate duplicate rows, exact normalized binary and unchanged empty status. Initial test compile attempted private to_header_map; corrected to public add_header. cargo check --locked passes (~4s); rustfmt applied. Five existing eventsource-stream warnings remain. No new runner/scripts/native release build/clipboard operations. This proves the status adapter and compilation, not actual HTTP2 call routing, trailers-only wire, WebView/history/provider/platform or complete gRPC parity; those remain open.



## Radix-string rounding correction — 2026-10-16

Legacy numeric conversion now rounds binary/octal/hex integer strings once to binary64 using a bounded significand, round bit and sticky bit. The former f64 digit accumulator could round repeatedly: 0x1000000000000081 produced an incorrect double/int32, while actual pinned protobuf.js7.2.4 gives wire 09010000000000b043108002 (int32 256). Three radix representations and two ties-to-even controls match actual fromObject/encode bytes. Empty/invalid/overflow/leading-zero controls are in one focused Rust regression; existing numeric-whitespace and JSON-overflow regressions also pass. Decimal/Long parsing and strict ProtoJSON code are unchanged. Constant auxiliary storage; existing input limits remain.

Validation: cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --lib legacy_radix_rounding_matches_protobufjs_wire exits0 (one test; compile/link45.71s, execution0.00s). The same compiled test binary runs the two existing affected regressions with exit0 in0.01s. No new artifact/UI scripts, native release build, profile or network calls. Native editor/wire/platform acceptance and broader migration remain open. References: https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/abstract-operations.html#sec-stringtonumber and installed pinned protobufjs7.2.4/src/converter.js in the existing grpc-legacy-reference environment.

## JSON-only primitive conversion — 2026-10-16

Legacy conversion now rejects own non-callable toString at string/numeric conversion boundaries, including joined arrays, Long low/high and consumed byte length/indices. Non-coercing bool/enum/message values and unused byte indices remain accepted. No UI/layout or production dependency change. The262row pinned protobufjs7.2.4/proto-loader0.7.13/Long5.3.2 oracle and full74Rust tests pass; native58controls/34refusals passed with184Save exports and exact profiles. See STATUS.md for evidence and the deliberately interrupted wider regression. Full-suite/post-suite acceptance is not claimed.

To avoid repeating the unit matrix through every UI lifecycle, native defaults select16positive/12refusal integration representatives. Set INSOMNIUM_GRPC_OBJECT_PRIMITIVE_MATRIX=1 only when the full native matrix is justified. The262row Rust matrix remains exhaustive within its authored scope. Broader legacy compatibility and provider/platform requirements remain open. Primary conversion reference: https://tc39.es/ecma262/2024/#sec-ordinarytoprimitive .

## Current native legacy byte-array-like conversion — 2026-10-16

Legacy bytes zero-wrapped lengths and sparse repeated tails have bounded Windows acceptance:30new actual editor/Format/mode-switch/wire/JSON/history/reload/Save cases (15saved-file/15reflection) plus4zero-additional-IO refusal/durable-error/typed-repair cases retain all245prior groups. Full73Rust tests match124pinned byte-array-like rows (110success/14refusals), preserve original descriptor pools and retain strict ProtoJSON rejection/canonical Base64 controls. Fresh24native-hidden scenarios/279groups/502actual successful gRPC Save exports/exact24profiles and original0override source/profile/export closure pass. Broader JSON/object-coercion/map/template/editor/stream/provider/platform and every retained PLAN/PARITY gate remain open.

Legacy conversion now applies the existing uint32 operation to an array-like length before copying: zero-wrapped positive lengths, including positive numeric overflow and string Infinity, emit an explicit empty bytes field without large allocation. Nonzero oversized lengths retain the20MiB refusal. protobuf.js fromObject only assigns valid repeated byte entries by index: invalid trailing entries do not extend the array; an invalid slot followed by a valid value would leave a hole that BufferWriter cannot write, so Rust refuses that request. Enum and private string-view conversion remain separate; source-specific file/reflection JSON modes and standard ProtoJSON descriptors are unchanged. No production dependency or UI/layout change.

Actual pinned protobufjs7.2.4/proto-loader0.7.13 requestSerialize/responseDeserialize produced108initial rows and46Rust RED divergences (42wrong size refusals,4wrong repeated empty writes). Expanded124rows retain the exact108row prefix and add leading/middle/trailing/all-invalid sparse controls. Final full73Rust tests pass0failed/0filtered; original pools remain byte-identical and strict ProtoJSON rejects every array-like object while canonical Base64 still works. This is a finite bounded matrix, not a complete ToPrimitive or all-object-coercion claim.

Native30positive cases retain raw1e999/large-integer tokens through actual editor Format/mode switching and prove exact wire/JSON/history/reload/Save for ordinary/repeated/nested values, file defaults/reflection sparsity, trailing omissions and valid fractional/small controls. Four additional native cases prove leading-hole/nonzero-wrap refusal before additional peer IO, exact error reload, actual typed repair, diagnostic clearing and success/error history retention across both origins. Refusal Save checks now use the previously accepted exact-history byte-count policy; full malformed JSON still fails. All192prior authored positive specs/default14selection remain unchanged. Strict7JS, frontend0errors/0warnings, native check and real loopback30business/60discovery/60sessions/60sockets pass. The initial strict union-access error was corrected to the authored profile kind before build.

## Current native reflection dependency/cache arrival — 2026-10-16

Reflection dependency batching and shared-cache arrival have bounded Windows acceptance:19new actual editor/wire/JSON/history/reload/Save cases (16declaration/arrival/cache combinations and3transitive controls) retain all226prior groups. Full71Rust tests include real HTTP2 partial-reply peers, wrong/foreign/missing/duplicate reply refusals, a1000file cached chain and shared4096query budget. Fresh22native-hidden scenarios/245groups/434actual successful gRPC Save exports/exact22profiles and original manifest plus explicit1test/1STATUSdoc supplemented source/profile/export closure pass. Broader Any/JSON/template/editor/stream/provider/platform and every retained PLAN/PARITY gate remain open.

The existing gRPC transport delegates discovery to grpc_reflection.rs. Each version attempt shares descriptor cache and query/received-byte/file budgets across concurrent advertised-service roots. Missing filename requests form a separate correlated bidi batch; cached files precede new files in actual response order, and every batch drains through stream completion. Parent descriptors precede child continuations; cached-only continuations use FIFO rather than scheduler-dependent yields. Iterative traversal handles deep cached chains. Service results retain original advertised order and scoped returned extras. Source-specific legacy/file/ProtoJSON behavior, method indexes, metadata/TLS and UI layout remain in place; no production dependencies changed.

Pinned grpc-reflection-js0.3.0/proto-loader0.7.13/protobufjs7.2.4 matrix16rows exposed6serial-fetch divergences. Actual HTTP2 peers produced2RED/1PASS before implementation. A subsequent cached-transitive pinned control exposed d/c/e versus required c/d/e despite the first green suite; FIFO fixed it without changing the oracle. Cold deep-first c/e/d and shallow-first d/c/e controls verify exact distinct Any wire. Malformed reply correlation refuses foreign/duplicate/missing original requests and wrong answer kinds. Final full71Rust tests pass0failed/0filtered. Initial older smoke any.proto-fetch assertion did not apply to these root packets and was replaced with actual left.proto/c.proto query provenance.

Saved native19cases cover declaration order, response order and none/left/right/both cache states plus3transitive controls. Seed gates use verified owned stream closure; cold branch replies use explicit40ms provider scheduling. Response telemetry records actual send order. Existing22service-root discovery assertions now prove separate root/dependency streams and shared-cache provenance rather than requiring per-root refetch; all prior wire/JSON/editor/history/Save guards remain. All173prior authored specs/default14selection remain unchanged. Original22service and33profile loopback smoke remain green.

## Current native gRPC JSON refusal and repair

<a id="current-native-grpc-json-refusal"></a>

Four bounded Windows native-hidden cases verify malformed JSON in legacy/protoJson and a valid message with an unrecognized field in each mode. Legacy accepts the unknown field without rewriting the authored request; the wire/response omit it according to its explicit legacy contract. ProtoJSON refuses the unknown field in its native parser. Both modes reject malformed JSON during renderer preflight with a visible SyntaxError and no fabricated call history.

Three refusals show a diagnostic with zero additional observed fixture streams/sessions/sockets. Renderer malformed-JSON refusal creates no call history before/after reload and retains the authored body. Native unknown-field refusal creates one durable error entry with no sent/received payload; reload preserves it exactly and does not dispatch. Actual mounted/focused CodeMirror keyboard repair followed by explicit Load methods/Send produces a successful history, status0 and the exact independently authored19byte int64/bytes frame. Native error and all success histories survive reload unchanged; unrelated histories remain exact. Actual Manage/Delete removes all owned history and cannot resurrect it. This is owned-peer evidence, not a universal absence-of-network-side-effects claim.

Host58900 exits0/native-hidden/profile1792074785118 restores exactly and failed closure passes. Matcher is corrected to the actual diagnostic, retaining zero-peer-IO/history/repair assertions and requiring the diagnostic to clear after success. No application change is claimed.

Corrected legacy Number/bitwise coercion to trim exactly ECMAScript WhiteSpace+LineTerminator rather than Rust Unicode whitespace. Added13actual LegacyValue serializer vectors RED101/GREEN0, including -0, exponent thresholds, subnormal/max, NaN/infinities and float0.1. Corrected F32/F64 output with existing ryu-js through validated borrowed serde_json RawValue. Only raw_value feature enabled; no dependency version or lockfile change. Standard ProtoJSON unchanged. Output evidence: artifacts/grpc-number-output-reference.json and grpc-number-output-red/green-all logs with exit files.

Four Windows native-hidden cases cover NEL/NBSP/BOM/ZWSP string inputs in both optional int32 and double fields. Real mounted editor typing, Format and JSON mode flip/back preserve authored strings; independent16byte frames distinguish integer0/doubleNaN from integer1/double1. Optional int32 presence and legacy _value selector are checked. Pinned protobuf.js independently agrees on full wire bytes and response values/selector. Existing serializer key order remains an open compatibility gate and is not normalized or claimed equivalent by these cases. Responses/history/full authored bodies survive reload and actual JSON Save before/after reload; owned Manage/Delete prevents resurrection, unrelated histories remain exact. Eight new exports pass entire-object equality checks.

## Current legacy Long whitespace compatibility

<a id="current-native-grpc-long"></a>

Corrected decimal Long parsing to use the same ECMAScript whitespace predicate as Number conversion, retaining eight UTF16-unit parseInt chunks and sign behavior. Rust previously trimmed U+0085 NEL inside a chunk, incorrectly converting NEL+1+NEL to1instead of0 and12345678+NEL+1to1234567801instead of1234567800. Pinned protobuf.js7.2.4/Long5.3.2 reference34vectors covers25whitespace/line terminators,4non-whitespace controls and5chunk/sign cases. Actual production long_bits helper RED101/GREEN0 and all21Rust library tests pass; existing29numeric/13float-output/37Base64 vectors remain passing. Evidence artifacts/grpc-long-whitespace-reference.json and grpc-long-whitespace-red/green-all logs with exit files. No dependency change.

Six Windows native-hidden cases cover NEL/NBSP, chunk NEL/NBSP and negative NEL/BOM in both optional int64/uint64. Real mounted editor typing/Format/JSON mode flip preserve authored strings. Independent wire literals match the pinned reference; responses retain exact signed/unsigned decimal strings, explicit zero presence and both optional selectors, including signed-1/unsigned18446744073709551615. Complete body/history/reload and twelve actual JSON exports before/after reload pass; Manage/Delete cannot resurrect owned histories and unrelated histories remain exact. Existing JSON key ordering is not claimed equivalent; broader ordering remains open.

## Current legacy JSON numeric overflow compatibility

The legacy-only Json tree uses validated borrowed RawValue captures and f64 numeric nodes; no global arbitrary_precision/JSON configuration or dependency change. ProtoJSON stays separate. ## Current legacy oneof member preservation

<a id="current-native-grpc-oneof"></a>

Legacy protobuf.js retains multiple oneof members, including explicit0/empty-string values. The prior parser rejected these; simply removing its guard would let prost discard members. Legacy-only runtime descriptor views now give members independent presence groups, preserving original field numbers/types and synthetic optional ordering. Original schema/reflection descriptors stay unchanged. Incoming decoding uses the same legacy view; serialization carries original descriptor context through nested messages/lists/maps and emits one original-name selector for the highest present field number. ProtoJSON uses original mutual-exclusion descriptors. Generated internal names avoid fields, oneofs, nested types, enum types/values and message extensions. No custom marker option, global registry or dependency change.

Actual parser regression RED101/GREEN0; full27Rust library tests pass, covering conflicting/reversed/default input, both incoming wire/declaration orders, single selector occurrence, optional0, immutable/idempotent descriptors, nested list/map and standard ProtoJSON refusal/last-member decode. A separate actual valid-schema collision regression RED101 then GREEN covers enum values and message extensions with suffix chains. Pinned protobuf.js7.2.4 references: artifacts/grpc-oneof-reference.json (13cases) and grpc-oneof-collision-reference.json (2valid schemas/wire only). Extension JSON/default parity and general JSON property order remain open.

## Authority and lifetime

`src/lib/grpc-model.js` registers metadata only for the actual existing filename Error inside `validateProtoFiles`. A private WeakMap associates the same Error object with frozen `{kind,name,rows}` metadata; exported `protoFilenameIssue(error)` returns null for an unregistered error. Backend strings or similarly shaped errors cannot gain a field owner. Duplicate indices are determined only when the existing duplicate branch actually fails. Count first, ordered filename-before-size checks, per-file/aggregate UTF8 limits, exact messages and post-loop file/directory collision checks remain unchanged. The same existing Error instance propagates through import/prepare change.

ProtoManager captures the submitted pending values, request ID, raw data identity and epoch. Only a current matching batch accepts a branded refusal; disposal, changed raw data/request/epoch or different current pending values decline it. A separate filename owner leaves global error state untouched. On edit it runs the actual complete validator: retain/update only the same kind and row ownership; otherwise retire the old owner without automatically publishing another unsubmitted issue. New selection/discard/success and actual owner retirement leave no stale local association. Existing key/tab/read cancellation and originating-picker focus guards remain unchanged.

Local display:contents and the existing five-pixel input-margin selector preserve normal geometry. Field feedback uses a local block/overflow-wrap:anywhere rule after a real long-error negative: a900-character name gave width5536.765625/right5552.765625 against previewright1432 at1440. The immutable `long-error-negative` captures source/output/log. The correction wraps within existing preview padding without changing shared feedback styles/font/color. Current1440 measurement width1408/right1424/height90 fits; all six widths/themes pass strict bounds.

[ECMAScript WeakMap specification](https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/keyed-collections.html#sec-weakmap-objects) governs private object-identity lookup; [Svelte lifecycle](https://svelte.dev/docs/svelte/lifecycle-hooks) and installed Field/context remain the existing rendering contracts. <details>
<summary>GRPC-COMPATIBILITY</summary>

# gRPC implementation and compatibility

2026-09-24. Native transport, legacy JSON and Svelte integration are implemented and directly inspected. Actual Tauri UI/IPC acceptance and full gRPC parity remain incomplete. BUILD.json is the authority for the last verified package.

## Current native contract

- `load_grpc_schema({request:{id,proto,connection,jsonMode},onStarted})` uses explicit proto input or server reflection. The required Channel receives null after registry insertion, so cancellation can retry safely. Proto input is `{entry,files:[{name,text}],includePaths}`. Names must be relative slash paths without dot/parent segments. Resolution checks exact name, explicit include directories, then built-in Google protos. No arbitrary disk/network imports. Returns base64 FileDescriptorSet and method path/input/output/streaming flags/default example. An unconstructible Any example has `exampleError` and does not hide the method.
- `connect_grpc({request:{id,connection,descriptorSet,method,body,jsonMode},onEvent})` validates the descriptor/method. Unary and server-streaming parse/send body once, then close their sending side. Client/bidi streams start with no messages. `send_grpc_message({id,text,finish:false})` sends one message; `{id,text:null,finish:true}` closes only the sending side. Call results continue until final status/error/cancel/deadline. The captured jsonMode is used for subsequent messages.
- `connection` includes URL, ordered metadata pairs, timeoutMs and optional caPem/identityPem. grpc/raw authority uses plaintext; grpcs uses TLS. HTTP/S schemes are also accepted. Host is parsed normally; method path is case-sensitive. Paths, userinfo, queries and fragments are rejected. Connection/schema timeout is clamped to1ms–1hour. Renderer call deadlines are separate (default0 means none); see the independent call deadlines section. TLS uses webpki roots, optional CA and combined certificate/private-key PEM. Proxy support remains unimplemented. Certificate validation follows the global/on/off request setting; see the current certificate-validation controls section.
- Events are `{event,sequence}` through a Tauri Channel: started, connected, metadata/headers, message/text, status/code/details/trailers. Every event awaits `acknowledge_grpc({id,sequence})`; acknowledgement timeout30s. Started permits cancellation retry after registry insertion. Outbound queue capacity1; sends and half-close are serialized. `cancel_http({id})` uses the shared registry and drops the live future. Registry cleanup covers ordinary terminal paths; poisoned-lock cleanup attempts all registries.
- Metadata permits128 rows/256KiB combined; names use gRPC characters, text must be printable ASCII. Protocol header overrides are rejected. Binary `-bin` input accepts padded/unpadded base64; responses split comma-joined values and normalize to padded base64. Invalid response binary values produce an error. Nonzero status retains separate grpc-status-details-bin bytes.
- Reflection uses generated v1 message types and wire-compatible v1alpha path after Unimplemented. Requests/responses are correlated, transitive imports fetched, duplicate descriptors checked, and listed services must exist in the resulting pool. Limits:1024 services/files,4096 queries,8MiB aggregate descriptor bytes. An empty service list is allowed.
- Limits:256 proto files/include directories,2MiB per source/8MiB total,8MiB descriptor and method-description budgets,4096 methods,20MiB wire and rendered JSON messages. JSON output is bounded during serialization. Local compilation runs on a blocking worker; cancelling the wait does not interrupt that worker.

## JSON modes and remaining differences

- Default `legacy` follows the archived protobuf.js7.2.4/Long5.3.2 fromObject/toObject pipeline: original field names, JS-like coercions, ignored unknown fields, defaults, decimal string longs, enum names and virtual oneof discriminators. Known Any values and WKT object forms are handled. Bytes render as numeric-key objects, matching Buffer becoming Uint8Array across Electron IPC before JSON.stringify. Int64 map keys follow the original eight-character hash representation. Proto2 required/default handling includes the original large-number rounding behavior.
- Explicit `protoJson` uses prost-reflect standard ProtoJSON parsing/WKT/base64 bytes, emitted defaults and original field names on output. Unknown input fields fail; virtual oneof discriminator fields are absent. Switching modes never rewrites saved bodies.
- Native prost-reflect continues to encode/decode protobuf; grpc_legacy.rs adapts JSON only. ryu-js supplies ECMAScript number formatting. No frontend protobuf.js dependency, eval permission or binary IPC mode was added. Reference-only libraries are under ignored artifacts/grpc-legacy-reference.
- Current boundary (reconciled 2026-10-18): later source and receipts in this document implement bounded deterministic Base64 variants, UTF-16 surrogate handling, numeric/object/array-like coercions, map ordering/collisions, known proto2 extensions/default escapes, reflection/custom Value descriptors and multiple populated oneof members. The historical blanket limitations for those families no longer describe current source. Their recorded controls do not prove all inputs/profiles or native editor/Send/history/export/reload behavior. Nondeterministic unwritten legacy Base64/writer bytes remain explicitly refused; unknown extensions are ignored. Continue remaining JSON/Any/shared-editor/template/stream/provider/platform requirements against a concrete source/reference discrepancy or a named missing acceptance gate.

## Svelte workflow

- September28 method presentation: package-grouped optgroups preserve discovery order and full-path values, with shortened labels/full-path tooltips and all four RPC kind labels. Retained sent messages also appear as Body/Stream N request tabs with timestamps/read-only content; selected event IDs safely fall back to Body if absent.10 inline grouping checks, Svelte0/0 and frontend build passed. Native keyboard/picker/tab interaction remains unverified. These frontend changes are newer than the last packaged BUILD.json.

- Original request layout now routes grpc_request to GrpcPane: URL, proto/reflection and method selection, body/metadata/proto/docs/settings tabs, send/cancel, client-stream Send message/Commit, unary response tabs and timestamped stream log with auto-scroll. History, metadata/trailers and JSON download reuse bounded persisted state.
- File/directory selection previews relative names before additive import into a fresh Imported protos directory. ProtoManager renders a keyed nested list with replace/refresh/delete actions. Refresh maps paths relative to the chosen saved folder, keeps IDs/unknown fields and absent old files, and validates every file in the affected root before commit. File replacement can update the basename while preserving its ID. Duplicate/file-directory collisions fail before mutation. Validation cancellation or changed saved protos discard the candidate; unrelated request edits survive. Source remains directly editable while repairing invalid legacy input.
- Subtree removal shows file count/affected requests and requires explicit in-app confirmation. All requests retain visible missing-file references after deletion. Replacement validation participates in shutdown/running tracking. Disk-save failures explicitly report in-memory changes; they are not claimed as durable. Selected top-level proto directories resolve in isolation; duplicate filenames from separate imports cannot leak across roots. Native picker/runtime directory behavior still needs verification.
- URL/metadata/body use the active environment; disabled metadata is skipped. Client/bidi initial connection skips body parsing until Send message. Global CA and exact-host TLS identity are supported; ordinary HTTP authentication/cookies/proxy are not applied. Schema cache is capped at three entries/20MiB; context hashes include relevant schema/settings/rendered values, not body. Endpoint/proto/environment/method changes cancel active calls. Local proto discovery works without an endpoint.
- Schema discovery and calls join running/completion tracking, cancellation and application shutdown. Start callbacks retry cancellation after native registration. Deleting a request cannot restore its response history after a successful close. Native-only browser guidance is explicit.

Includes a legacy-mode bidi wire round trip with int64/bytes/Timestamp/oneof.
- 20 schema/metadata/bounds cases: Any discovery, scalar/enum/map/list defaults, unknown enum numbers, bad enum names, traversal/absolute names, malformed/cyclic imports, file/count/descriptor/message limits and metadata validation.
- 6 TLS cases: custom CA and mutual TLS successful over ALPN h2; untrusted certificate, wrong hostname, missing client identity and malformed identity rejected. Two successful TLS application calls. Synthetic RSA key was written only to a unique ignored fixture path for native OpenSSL certificate creation and removed immediately; servers were stopped.
- 97 differential legacy cases passed:81 coercion/message cases and16 proto2/WKT cases against pinned protobuf.js/Long, wire decode, structuredClone and JSON.stringify. Covers bounds, longs, bytes, enums, defaults, maps, Any and required fields; does not establish all edge parity.
- 75 frontend/workspace checks passed (including the original33), using actual Svelte-compiled workspace and grpc.js wrappers with mocked Tauri calls: context/cache, rendering, import/root isolation, streaming/half-close, cancellation registration, history deletion, shutdown and browser guidance. Added refresh/rename/merge retention, collision rejection, failed validation, concurrent saved-proto edits, independent request edits, subtree removal, save failure and cancellation/shutdown during validation. Real compiled Rust modules supplied schema compilation for valid refresh, broken unchanged-service dependency rejection and rename. This does not verify real IPC.
- Final Svelte check0/0, Prettier and git diff --check passed. Native source is unchanged from the prior clippy -D warnings/fmt checkpoint; earlier dependency regression checks passed89 ASAP plus14 manual-auth/Digest/OAuth1 cases. No saved test scripts. Current proto-manager snapshot artifacts/grpc-proto-source-snapshot.json contains178 files; packaging outcome is recorded separately in STATUS/BUILD.json.

## Native implementation

- grpc_schema.rs: protox0.9.1 memory-only resolver, explicit include directories plus Google well-known types; prost-reflect0.16.5/prost0.14.4 dynamic codec. Up to256 files,2MiB each/8MiB aggregate,4096 methods,20MiB protobuf/JSON messages. No external protoc or filesystem import resolution. Default legacy mode delegates JSON adaptation to grpc_legacy.rs; explicit protoJson remains available. Known edge differences are in GRPC.md#record-grpc-compatibility.
- grpc_transport.rs: tonic0.14.6 channel with TLS roots/optional CA/identity; four RPC shapes through generic streaming dispatch with schema cardinality checks. Text/binary metadata, statuses/trailers, gzip decoding. Reflection v1 with Unimplemented fallback to v1alpha and bounded transitive descriptors. Protocol header overrides rejected. No proxy or disabled-certificate-validation option yet.
- grpc.rs: load_grpc_schema/connect_grpc/send_grpc_message/acknowledge_grpc; shared cancel_http network registry and deadlines. One outbound queued message; each incoming event awaits sequence acknowledgement. Finish only closes the sender. Local proto compilation runs on a blocking worker; cancellation stops waiting but cannot interrupt an already running compiler thread.
- Official sources additionally read: https://docs.rs/protox/latest/protox/struct.Compiler.html , https://docs.rs/prost-reflect/latest/prost_reflect/struct.DynamicMessage.html , https://docs.rs/tonic/latest/tonic/struct.Streaming.html , https://protobuf.dev/programming-guides/json/ , https://github.com/grpc/grpc-proto/blob/master/grpc/reflection/v1/reflection.proto and v1alpha/reflection.proto . Installed crate source confirms generated reflection types are available without server feature.
- Final clippy/fmt and Svelte check passed. Protocol/TLS, differential and mocked-IPC evidence is in GRPC.md#record-grpc-compatibility. Tauri IPC/UI remains unverified; BUILD.json records the last verified release separately from source progress.

## Follow-up inventory during ES512 packaging

Read the remaining main/ipc/grpc.ts: unary/server streaming send an initial JSON body; client/bidi start an empty outbound stream, accept explicit sendMessage actions and then commit to close only the sending side. The old nextTick write workaround and incomplete error cleanup should not be copied literally. Metadata rows retain order and disabled flags; binary metadata needs explicit treatment because the original passes strings.

parseGrpcUrl lowercases input, strips grpc:// for plaintext or grpcs:// for TLS, and treats an unprefixed address as plaintext. Preserve service/method case in the migration. The original native client uses createSsl defaults or createInsecure; this path does not pass the HTTP custom-CA/client-identity settings.

write-proto-file reconstructs saved trees in a temporary cache and uses every created subdirectory as an include directory, making sibling-name resolution potentially ambiguous. ProtoFile stores name/protoText; GrpcRequestMeta stores pinned/lastActive. Prefer a bounded in-memory resolver if supported, preserving saved hierarchy and validating imported names.

common/render.ts renders the request with description separately and optional skipBody, then each client-stream message independently. grpc-response-pane shows unary responses in tabs and streaming responses in timestamped stacked JSON editors with auto-scroll and a status/details header. Method changes are checked separately.

Optional ignored fields still remain absent; accepted explicit values and error paths retain their behavior. This applies to saved-file and reflection Legacy profiles; strict ProtoJSON is unchanged.

## gRPC certificate-validation controls — 2026-10-18

Source parentc8e02367. gRPC Settings now exposes the existing global/on/off TLS certificate-validation choice. grpcConnection resolves the request override before the global preference (default true), refuses invalid override values, forwards validateCertificates to native code and includes the authored setting in grpcSourceContext. Resolved connection/schema identity also contains it, preventing stale cached reflection or in-flight context reuse after a trust-setting change. Matching-host client identity selection remains active when verification is skipped.

Native Connection defaults missing validateCertificates to true for older callers. The common channel used by application calls and v1/v1alpha reflection applies ordinary webpki/custom CA verification when enabled. Explicit false uses documented Endpoint::tls_config_with_verifier with a private rustls verifier that skips server chain/name/time trust checks, while delegating TLS1.2/1.3 handshake signature verification and supported schemes to the ring provider. It does not disable encryption, SNI, HTTP2 ALPN or client-certificate authentication. Custom CA inputs are not loaded or mixed into the custom-verifier path; the same identity loader and1MiB guard remain. No new dependencies, lockfile changes or custom socket/HTTP2 dispatcher.

Primary references read: https://docs.rs/tonic/0.14.6/tonic/transport/struct.Endpoint.html#method.tls_config_with_verifier ; https://docs.rs/rustls/0.23.45/rustls/client/danger/trait.ServerCertVerifier.html ; locked tonic channel/endpoint.rs and channel/service/tls.rs documents the incompatibility of custom verifiers with root/CA methods and retained SNI/identity behavior. Existing UI/layout is retained, with the familiar TLS selector in the existing Settings panel and corrected help text.

## Independent gRPC call deadlines — 2026-10-18

Source parent92026eed. The global connection timeout previously also terminated every gRPC call and supplied grpc-timeout, including long-lived streams. Current renderer requests now carry a separate grpcDeadlineMs setting (0 by default = no call deadline, otherwise integral1–3,600,000ms). The existing Settings panel exposes it. Invalid/negative/fractional/nonfinite/out-of-range values refuse before dispatch. Both raw and resolved source identities include the setting. Import/export retains it as ordinary request data. Archived main/ipc/grpc.ts creates unary/client/server/bidi calls without explicit deadline options, consistent with the gRPC default described at https://grpc.io/docs/guides/deadlines/ .

Native Connection accepts optional deadlineMs: omitted retains the previous timeoutMs-based behavior for old IPC clients, explicit0 removes the call deadline, positive values clamp to1ms–1hour. A shared call_deadline accessor supplies both the local IPC timeout and Tonic Request::set_timeout. Zero omits the wire grpc-timeout header and bypasses only the local whole-call timeout. Connection establishment and reflection/schema lookup still use the bounded global timeout; ACK deadlines, user cancellation, close/drain and registry cleanup retain their paths. Reflection does not inherit an unlimited call deadline. No dependency/native-command changes.

## gRPC remaining deadline propagation — 2026-10-18

Source parentb97812c5. Finite call deadlines previously restarted the full configured grpc-timeout after connection and renderer delivery waits. connect_grpc now computes one monotonic absolute Instant before Started delivery, uses timeout_at for the whole call and passes that same Instant to the internal transport call. After channel readiness, metadata, Connected callback and codec preparation, the transport sends only the positive remaining duration. Exhausted budgets return the existing deadline error before application RPC dispatch. No-deadline calls still omit grpc-timeout; connection/schema/ACK/cancellation policy is unchanged. No new IPC fields or dependencies.

## Custom gRPC DNS servers and alternative resolution — 2026-10-19

Frontend/native now validate and retain numeric DNS-server authorities in dns://server[:port]/host[:port], including IPv6 and TLS wrappers. ## Configured gRPC retry implementation stage — 2026-10-19

Source parent b66fe32a. RPC and reflection now share configured pre-initial-metadata retry execution, max5attempts, jittered/capped backoff, pushback, previous-attempt metadata, remaining wire deadline and owned replay cancellation. Payload replay budgets are1MiB/call and16MiB/shared resolution handle, with zero-wire-message run storage; accepted queued messages are staged before attempts/readiness completion so already-queued oversized unary payloads disable replay without dropping outbound messages. Target throttle buckets publish with accepted resolution config, round to3decimals, retain old-call bucket ownership and rescale on updates. Response-header observation commits before Tonic compression validation; trailers-only encoding is deferred then restored to final metadata. This is an implementation stage, not full retry conformance.

Known remaining timing gap: producer enqueue is not itself an allocation event; messages accepted during an active attempt/backoff are accounted when body polling or next-attempt staging runs. Reference buffer exhaustion during backoff can disable retries before a throttle denial whereas this stage can retain the completed status; exact enqueue-time/global-budget ordering needs follow-up. Hedging, transparent NOT_STARTED/REFUSED progress, raw original HEADERS END_STREAM distinctions, LB, canonical target/channel lifecycle, strict JSON/extreme-calendar compatibility and native/provider/platform acceptance remain open. No full migration gate removed.

## Shared native round-robin pool and dispatch reservations — 2026-10-19

Source parent15df7ee9. Added grpc_lb_transport.rs native Channel wrapper and shared round-robin pool. When unproxied named-DNS resolution already has a selected round_robin policy, actual RPC/reflection now use one strict unbuffered ReadyConnection per complete DNS address. Other routes retain buffered Channel through the common wrapper; no P2C substitution. Per-resolution list is rotated by the pinned random signed32 start index before child creation. Shared ready-set ring preserves its next endpoint when present, defaults to first ready when absent, and resets when a pick observes no ready entries. Cloned clients/retries/hedges share the owner/ring but never clone a live reservation.

Each dispatch reserves the exact service via an owned async mutex guard; the guard remains from readiness until call and releases immediately after dispatch, not across response consumption. A reserved subchannel lost before dispatch produces a typed native NOT_STARTED marker, retaining any original transport source, so transparent replacement repicks without consuming configured attempts/throttle. No ready children plus Connecting waits for revision; no ready/connecting children returns unavailable. Initial pool construction waits for an actual Ready child under the existing outer DNS timeout without consuming a ring turn.

Per-child owner task drives initial connect/reconnect on the original TLS/authority/settings/timeout stack, watches connectivity while idle and publishes revisions. It owns only a slot/observer/revision sender, never Entry/Owner; Entry Drop aborts it. Reconnect uses pinned defaults1second,1.6multiplier,120second pre-jitter cap,20percent jitter, starts its delay before the handshake and waits only the remaining interval after failure; Ready resets it. Node fractional-millisecond truncation is retained. Drop/abort is a source ownership design, not measured await/drain/physical FIN. Added ReadyConnection lazy endpoint API using strict mode; normal buffered/lazy APIs remain unchanged. No dependency/lock changes.

## Late TXT policy routing and response ownership — 2026-10-19

Source parent88632055. ResolutionConfig now publishes owned revision notifications for target reset/accepted TXT-result publication; tasks capture snapshot/watch sender rather than Inner. Named unproxied DNS channels gain a shared dynamic route owner, so TXT selecting round_robin after initial connect can construct/install the native pool, and a later default/pick_first selection can construct the existing buffered transport. Initial target generation is captured before dialing. New route publication validates generation, revision and desired policy under the snapshot read guard before synchronously updating the route; stale builds are cancelled/dropped and never installed. The route-owner task captures state/config/factories, not its handle owner; owner Drop aborts it.

Pending route construction preserves the old route while CONNECTING. Pinned load-balancer-child-handler.ts promotes any non-CONNECTING pending child, including TRANSIENT_FAILURE; source review corrected an initial readiness-only approach before commit. Replacement RR pools now publish on Ready or all-disconnected; failed buffered replacement publishes Unavailable and retries under owned default backoff. No hidden healthy-old-route fallback after a failed replacement. Existing reservations remain on the exact old route; ungranted waiters subscribe to route revisions, release an obsolete pending reservation and repick. Granted services stay reserved through call, including across policy changes.

Response futures/identity map_frame bodies now retain the exact selected subchannel Entry (or buffered channel) until body drop, allowing old route owners to release unrelated subchannels while existing responses continue. Data/trailer frames and end-stream semantics are delegated unchanged. No mutex spans response consumption. This is source ownership design; actor abort/drain, wire continuity and physical FIN require direct evidence. Method policy/deadline selection remains per-call and is not mutated by late LB routing.

## Owned pool-driven DNS/TXT re-resolution — 2026-10-19

Source parent4d25a18c. Active RR pool health transitions now publish re-resolution requests when an observed parent had Ready children and a child transitions away from Ready, including another child's non-Ready transition while the parent is Ready. Shared health map serializes that decision; initial unchanged Connecting reports do not trigger requests. Generation-aware reporting infers a lost old connection when watch coalesces directly from Ready to a new Ready generation, so fast reconnect does not suppress the request.

Added grpc_lb_resolution.rs event-driven worker owned by the dynamic route owner. It subscribes to the current RR route's request stream, switches subscriptions on route epochs and retains the receiver through each lookup rather than replaying historical requests in a loop. A loss predating the initial subscription remains pending once. Requests during cooldown coalesce into one deferred lookup, and requests during an active lookup coalesce/are consumed after it. No periodic polling or DNS call while a healthy unchanged route is idle. The worker invokes the existing resolver, whose fresh successful canonical publication already updates policy/address revisions.

DNS records the latest lookup attempt start per target generation, including attempts that fail, and the worker waits until that start+30seconds before executing a requested update rather than returning a fresh cached result and silently losing the request. Target retirement cancels sleep/lookup; same-target TXT/address updates do not cancel pending address lookup. This is independent of route building, so TXT policy construction continues concurrently with DNS. Owner Drop aborts both policy and resolver tasks; neither task captures its JoinHandle owner. Drop/abort is source ownership, not measured task drain or physical DNS/FIN closure.

## DNS overlapping failure coalescing (2026-10-19)

Within each logical DNS resolver, callbacks waiting on an in-flight address lookup now consume the completed result, including its error, instead of each waiter issuing another lookup after a failure. A completion epoch is sampled before acquiring the existing lookup mutex; completion and cached result are published under that mutex. Successful answers retain the existing 30-second cache; a later independent request may retry an error. Cancellation releases the lookup mutex without publishing a partial completion. TXT publication, canonical addresses and target-generation guards remain unchanged. This covers overlapping callbacks of one resolver, not cross-owner target sharing or full resolver scheduling parity.

Full failure/backoff scheduling, pending-child authority, pick_first membership/reuse and all retained runtime/native/platform/weighted/outlier/migration gates remain open.

## DNS failure backoff scheduling (2026-10-19)

The shared per-resolver lookup cache now owns default backoff timing: 1000ms initial, 1.6 multiplier, 120000ms cap before +/-20 percent jitter, fractional milliseconds truncated. The clock begins at attempt start so a slow failure spends its own delay; success clears/reset backoff. A later independent connector/worker request waits only the remaining failure backoff. Overlapping callbacks still share the same completed answer/error. DNS failure replaces the successful 30-second minimum deadline with the failed attempt backoff deadline and retains previously published LB addresses. No unconditional failure polling/retry loop is introduced: another LB/connector request is required.

Resolution timing has its own watch signal, separate from policy/address revisions. The RR worker re-evaluates a deferred deadline when another lookup changes timing, including clearing the minimum timer on error; this does not cancel a pending policy build. Failure publication checks both target generation and exact latest attempt start. Target retirement remains handled by the existing owned-worker cancellation. ## Pending native RR re-resolution authority (2026-10-19)

The policy owner now publishes an explicit latest-child request subscription independently of the route used by RPCs. A pending RR pool starts and supplies its subscription before awaiting its first non-Connecting state; the old RR pool's source is revoked during replacement even while its route continues serving. A pending buffered policy publishes no RR source. Successful promotion preserves the same pending pool subscription, avoiding replay on route installation. If a superseded build returns to the current route, the current pool is re-subscribed with requests emitted while unauthorized discarded. Initial route history is retained once.

The resolver worker follows source changes with priority over old-pool signals and retains each subscription through its lookup. Already accepted DNS requests finish through cooldown/lookup unless target retirement cancels them; child source changes alone do not cancel accepted resolver work. A closed retired request stream waits for replacement publication instead of spinning. Source watches hold only receivers, not pool/Entry/route owners. Pending pool cancellation and Owner Drop keep the existing owned abort paths. ## Native pick_first DNS routing and membership (2026-10-19)

Unproxied named DNS targets now use native PickFirst over exact-reservation singleton subchannels instead of the buffered connect_resolved adapter. Its owned worker interleaves address families after policy shuffle, starts candidates at 250ms intervals, advances the latest candidate early on observed connection failure, selects an observed READY subchannel, drops competing candidate owners and retains the exact selected Entry through response bodies. Losing the current pick reports Idle until a call requests reconnect; repeated all-child failure reports sticky failure and requests re-resolution once each observed failure round. Native per-subchannel backoff remains owned by existing Pool workers.

Same-kind address/config publications update the live PickFirst owner. A READY selected address still in the list is reused before opening new connections; pending subchannels with matching addresses are reused on list updates. A removed selected connection continues serving while replacements connect, then yields to a READY replacement. Pending PickFirst and RR publish their own request subscriptions before first-state decisions; latest-child authority now includes native PickFirst, and removed buffered DNS reconnect callbacks no longer bypass it. Both pending policy types promote on non-Connecting state including failure and retain native retry owners, so obsolete policy-level buffered-build retry fallback was removed. Numeric/proxy transports remain on their existing buffered connector path. No frontend/IPC/dependency/lock/vendor changes.

## PickFirst cached READY order and pinned failure reset (2026-10-19)

Address updates now scan available current/pending native singleton pools in the new interleaved address order and choose the first cached READY subchannel before starting connections. The former current-pick shortcut could bypass an earlier READY pending candidate. Cached selection clears sticky failure just like pickSubchannel. This covers pools currently owned by this PickFirst instance, not a global/cross-policy subchannel cache.

Failure-round reset now follows the actual pinned grpc-js1.14.4 resetChildrenReportedTF expression: every(child=>child.hasReportedTransientFailure=false) short-circuits after the first false assignment, so only the first child resets. The native implementation had reset every child and therefore requested re-resolution on a different failure cadence. This intentionally preserves pinned behavior rather than assuming the method name describes its implementation. Initial policy revision also reconciles once after dialing, so a same-kind TXT/config revision arriving during initial connection is not incorrectly marked already applied.

## Coalesced failure detection and current-pick generation (2026-10-19)

Strict native Connectivity snapshots retain a wrapping failures counter across connection attempts. Central publication increments it only on a transition into Disconnected; duplicate Disconnected notifications do not increment, and generation-invalid driver observations still publish nothing. PickFirst compares the counter after an existing child observation baseline, so a Disconnected state followed by Connecting/Ready before the next sample no longer silently loses the observed failure flag/advance. A sampled Disconnected state still supplies the first-observation fallback. Counts detect at least one missed transition, not its full event history or every missed failure round.

Each selected/current PickFirst pool is now bound to the exact generation captured when its READY candidate was selected (including cached selection). Current state becoming non-READY or generation changing clears the pick, even if watch coalesced loss and a new READY connection into one latest snapshot. A new connection generation is therefore not silently treated as the old selected connection. Existing owned idle/demand/retry paths continue; cache retention/lifecycle remains a separate gate. ## Native connection failure versus established loss (2026-10-19)

Native connectivity now distinguishes TransientFailure from Disconnected. Connector readiness/connect errors and HTTP2 handshake errors publish TransientFailure; SendRequest readiness loss and established driver completion/drop retain Disconnected (native IDLE-equivalent). The failures counter now increments only on transitions into TransientFailure, deduplicates repeated failure notices and remains generation guarded. This supersedes the previous counter definition that counted every Disconnected transition. Established connection loss no longer falsely counts as a failed connection attempt or sets a pending PickFirst child's failure flag merely from a sampled Disconnected state. Current-pick removal still follows non-READY/generation loss; RR still treats both as non-READY for aggregate loss/re-resolution.

Existing native endpoint TimeoutConnector remains inside the observed connector future, so its connect error uses the same failure publication. No new connector, driver, task, queue, buffer or payload telemetry introduced. Vendor patch note records refined semantics; upstream LICENSE/dependency/lock/UI/IPC remain unchanged.

## PickFirst IDLE reconnect demand (2026-10-19)

Native singleton pools used by PickFirst now wait for an explicit reconnect request after established connection loss/Disconnected. The request carries that exact disconnected generation; an old request cannot wake a later connection generation. Pending PickFirst children request startConnecting when observed IDLE, while a lost current pick follows removal/idle/demand handling instead. Thus retaining Entry for a response body alone no longer authorizes an idle selected subchannel to reconnect. Pool owns the request sender; the driver task holds only a receiver, so sender closure ends an idle wait without an ownership cycle or additional task. Initial activation and owned candidate transient-failure retry remain driven by existing workers. RR retains eager idle reconnect behavior.

Retiring an established HTTP2 service can itself return a strict readiness error while resetting the internal reconnect service. That Disconnected result now resets the backoff delay and does not sleep/spend a new connection-failure backoff round. Actual connector/handshake TransientFailure continues using the existing start-relative exponential jitter timer. ## PickFirst continueConnecting and unrequested backoff expiry (2026-10-19)

Pending native PickFirst children now request startConnecting for both IDLE/Disconnected and TransientFailure. Singleton workers require the exact generation's request before post-failure continuation as well as idle reconnect; a prior generation's continuation cannot authorize another failure round. RR remains eager. Requests arriving during the existing start-relative backoff timer are retained until expiry, so the timer is not bypassed by demand.

When a PickFirst failure's backoff expires without a matching request, the owner uses guarded ReadyConnection::idle_after_failure to publish IDLE (native Disconnected) only if that same generation is still TransientFailure. READY, Shutdown, newer-generation and already-IDLE states are untouched. This read-only monitor operation does not connect, drop a driver or increment the failure counter. ## Endpoint-scoped native PickFirst subchannel cache (2026-10-19)

Initial and replacement PickFirst instances within one logical DNS route now share a SubchannelCache sealed to the factory's immutable Endpoint/authority/TLS/settings. SocketAddr (including IPv6 scope) identifies singleton pools inside that scope. Cache lookup returns a fresh per-consumer Pool wrapper over the same underlying owner/connection slot, never another consumer's readiness reservation. Removed/cancelled/idle pools can be reused by later PickFirst instances while the logical policy owner retains the cache, including a switch away from and back to PickFirst. This is local singleton caching, not global/cross-logical-channel or RR subchannel sharing.

The cache retains one owner reference, checks unused entries every10seconds, evicts entries with no other pool-owner references, stops its cleanup task when empty and restarts it on creation. The task captures only Weak cache ownership; final cache Drop aborts its cleanup handle and releases cached references. Active exact Entry/response-body ownership remains independent; source Drop/abort is not measured FIN/task-drain acceptance. READY cache lookup uses new-list order. Cached IDLE candidates follow sequential activation, cached TransientFailure candidates initialize failure flags and are skipped by initial connection sequence, and only state changes/activation/first sticky-failure transition request reconnect. This avoids waking every cached idle candidate at once.

## RR and PickFirst share the logical Endpoint cache (2026-10-19)

Native RR membership now obtains the same cached singleton owners/Entries used by PickFirst, preserving Endpoint/authority/TLS scope and exact connection reservations across kind switches and RR address-list rebuilds. RR retains its pinned random endpoint rotation and ready-set ring, while each RR owner has a separate membership observer, health map, update stream and re-resolution stream. Shared transport drivers accept generation-bound reconnect demand; RR membership keeps its children connected by issuing demand, rather than creating another driver/connection for each RR policy instance.

Initial health is seeded as one batch without treating cached initial states as losses from an already-READY parent. Singleton leaf failure stays logically TransientFailure through retry Connecting until READY, including observed counter-detected failures. Pending-policy failure decisions and no-ready readiness use logical leaf health; IDLE leaf queues while reconnect starts. Actual ready selection and granted service guards still verify the real transport state. The immutable Endpoint cache remains local to one logical DNS route, not global/cross-logical-channel sharing.

## Persistent pending native policy and applied revision barrier (2026-10-19)

The native PF/RR policy task now retains a pending Channel owner across observed same-kind config/address updates. Those updates revise the same pending controller and replace only its decision waiter; they no longer drop/recreate the child and its resolver subscription. A different observed pending kind revokes its source and replaces the pending child, including RR->pending PF->RR: a new latest RR child is built while the old current RR keeps serving. Promotion retains the pending request subscription. Source authority is revoked before constructing a replacement, and pre-subscription requests from the new child are retained once.

PF and RR address streams now carry per-owner revision tickets. Controllers publish applied tickets after processing membership/state; pending decisions wait for the latest ticket before checking non-Connecting state. This prevents a Ready/failed result from an older membership from promoting a pending child before its new address update has been processed. Config/target revision still guards route installation atomically; a rejected completed decision is re-armed safely. Stable current-policy updates remain in place, and already-granted service/body leases remain independent. Initial ticket0 follows each controller's initial decision state. All changes reuse existing tasks/watch channels with no new worker class or unbounded update queue.

## Resolver-driven retry throttler publication (2026-10-19)

Successful canonical address publication now installs its selected/default config and publishes a fresh target retry-throttler bucket before notifying config consumers. Even unchanged configs create a new bucket rescaled from the prior tokens, while already-selected Sessions retain the old Arc. ## Absolute packet acknowledgement budget (2026-10-19)

Replaced the PF-vs-RR boolean with PolicyKind across initial DNS route creation, pending/current child identity, supersession, same-kind update and atomic accepted-revision installation. Weighted/outlier selections no longer collapse to the same logical child identity as PickFirst: changing among those kinds now supersedes the pending child at the correct kind boundary. Address shuffling is explicitly limited to PickFirst. Existing PF/RR executors/service reservations remain; the prior placeholder PickFirst execution path for weighted/outlier is still incomplete and is not claimed as those executors. This is a routing prerequisite, not weighted/outlier acceptance or a replacement for implementing them.

Default per-RPC mode,10s blackout,180s expiration,1s weight refresh (minimum100ms), penalty1. The reference missing-weight queue period is mean positive weight (or1 if fewer than2positive), while positive weights use reciprocal period; do not silently replace that pinned behavior with a conventional scheduler. Heap parent uses floor(index/2), also requiring deliberate fidelity review. ## Weighted per-request ORCA picker implementation (2026-10-19)

WeightedRoundRobin now selects a native shared-endpoint pool on initial DNS and late pending policy construction, rather than the prior PickFirst placeholder. Same-kind updates retain policy-local endpoint metric state by SocketAddr; removed/re-added endpoint reports resolve against the current name map, and weak callback ownership cannot retain the policy controller. Existing exact connection grants/generation checks/response body ownership remain the transport boundary. Plain PF/RR have no weighted state or metric callback.

New grpc_lb_weighted.rs implements configured/default duration conversion with JS signed32 millisecond behavior,10s blackout/180s expiration/1s refresh(min100ms)/penalty1; per-call ORCA projection fields6rps_fractional/7eps/9application_utilization via existing Prost Message derive. The exact dispatched pool captures a policy metric sink; first endpoint-load-metrics-bin value from final trailers or trailers-only headers updates weights. Empty-zero weights are ignored; latest application utilization/error penalty formula, expiration resetting non-empty clock, and READY-generation blackout reset are implemented. READY picker rebuilds on connectivity/membership/refresh timer. Deadline-heap picks preserve the pinned positive reciprocal period, missing mean-positive period (or1), randomized phase, strict comparisons and unusual floor(i/2) parent operation. No conventional replacement scheduler or CPU-utilization fallback was invented.

Controller work reuses the existing owned RR membership task/cache/leaf health and adds a weighted refresh timer branch; no extra worker/buffer, dependency, lockfile, frontend or vendor change. Metric callbacks retain only config and a Weak current endpoint map; controller Drop and exact body/Entry retention remain existing Rust ownership, not measured FIN/task-drain proof. Config parsing/selected kind/accepted revision remain coherent; weighted config is read outside atomic snapshot publication guards to avoid nested-lock writer deadlock. Same-kind metric config changes affect current endpoint callbacks, as the reference's latestConfig lookup does.

## ORCA OOB endpoint producer implementation (2026-10-19)

Continues d4fe4ad6 weighted picker: native weighted policies now retain oob_load_reporting_period (default10000ms, same signed32 duration conversion), register endpoint watchers, and dispatch /xds.service.orca.v3.OpenRcaService/StreamCoreMetrics using the existing tonic-prost codec and protobuf Duration report_interval tag1. Existing per-request mode remains separate.

Each cached singleton Entry stores one ORCA producer. Policy registrations are keyed by endpoint address and survive retained membership; disabled OOB/removal drops them. Producer chooses the minimum requested interval, drops/replaces the streaming future on interval change, waits for endpoint READY, fans reports to current watchers, and suppresses future subscriptions after UNIMPLEMENTED until the producer is destroyed. CANCELLED does not arm retry; other errors use pinned1s initial,1.6 multiplier,120s base cap then20percent jitter without success reset. Clean OK completion retains a completed subscription until interval changes, matching reference metricsCall retention. Last registration removes producer and aborts worker, breaking its singleton-pool retention cycle; call/body destruction is the cancellation boundary. This is source ownership reasoning, not measured task/FIN drain.

## Outlier endpoint counters and ejection execution (2026-10-19)

Continues ORCA commit0889edcb. Initial DNS and dynamic pending outlier_detection now construct the native shared endpoint pool rather than PickFirst placeholder. Accepted policy payload retains outlier raw config and recursively selects its validated non-PF child, preserving nested outlier configuration layers and RR/weighted leaf. Each layer owns separate endpoint counters/ejection state, retained for present addresses in insertion order and removed for missing endpoints; in-flight call counters retain their originally selected entry rather than incrementing a replacement endpoint. Latest policy updates configure retained layers and RR/weighted leaf, and remove disabled layers/OOB weights. On RR-to-weighted leaf change, initial weight keys map current Entry addresses until membership rekeys them, avoiding rotating-RR index/name mismatch.

New grpc_lb_outlier.rs implements success/failure active/inactive buckets, counting-enabled interval scheduling, volume/minimum-host gates, mean/population standard deviation threshold with pinned multiplication grouping, strict failure-percentage threshold, random enforcement, current-percent cap, repeated ejection multipliers, strict expiry and multiplier decay. Defaults interval10000/base30000/max300000ms/cap10percent, success1900/100/5/100, failure85/100/5/50. Duration conversion retains JS signed32 behavior; nonpositive scheduled delays clamp1ms. Same-kind updates preserve timer-start elapsed time; enabling switches buckets, disabling cancels timer/unejects/resets multiplier. Interval checks reuse the existing owned membership task, no new worker. Ejected addresses are filtered from picker READY sets and projected unhealthy in policy reports without closing cached physical subchannels; weighted blackout state sees unhealthy/healthy transitions.

Exact selected pool dispatch captures counters with an unarmed observer; abandoning a readiness reservation does not count a call. Observer arms only after dispatch, counts canonical grpc-status0 success or non-OK once from final headers/trailers, and counts cancellation/transport error/missing terminal metadata as failure when the future/body drops. Existing response body/Entry retention and per-call ORCA callbacks remain. OOB internal singleton calls have no outlier layers, so are not counted as application calls. ## Nested child policy replacement ownership (2026-10-19)

Continues cf186a8b. Pool policy config now retains the complete selected kind path (each outlier wrapper and RR/weighted leaf). Dynamic route current/pending identity compares this path instead of only root Kind, so same-outer-kind nested/leaf changes construct a separate pending pool, retain the current route while pending Connecting, and publish on the existing non-Connecting decision/revision guard. A newer different path supersedes the pending route; same-path updates revise that pending owner. Resolver requests continue to follow only the latest pending/current source. No policy instance is now changed in place merely because its root kind stayed outlier_detection.

Outlier layers now use Arc<Mutex<Layer>> independently of pool ownership. New pending routes retain only the common leading outlier parent instances from the latest route (pending if present), configure those retained parents with latest settings, and allocate fresh deeper layers/leaf controller after the first kind difference. Counters/ejection multipliers/timer-start state of surviving parents persist while current and pending children overlap; retired inner instances remain separate. Both controllers can observe shared parent state, but each due check locks the layer and advances its deadline before another controller can execute that interval. Membership/counter access and pick masks now lock shared layers; no policy-owner back-reference is stored.

On successful route promotion the previous RR/weighted/outlier membership controller is explicitly aborted and releases weights/OOB registrations/outlier layer references, even if an old service reservation temporarily retains its Pool owner. PickFirst controller is likewise retired explicitly. Exact already-granted/dispatched Entry reservations and captured counter callbacks retain their independent lifetime; this source reasoning is not a measured cancellation/FIN/task-drain receipt. Pending supersession already drops its owned futures/pool and latest-source subscription. Shared physical singleton cache is retained through the existing cache boundary.

## Outlier picker count-enabled publication snapshots (2026-10-19)

Continues bbda2fc4. Binding now stores a count-enabled boolean captured at native picker publication; Call.new reads this snapshot and retains the original Stats counter, rather than upgrading Layer and reading latest configuration at every pick. Reference updateAddressList invokes child.updateAddressList before assigning latestConfig. Same-kind pool update similarly stages each previous layer flag. Bindings and the first applied-membership READY publication use those flags; an initial Connecting-only membership consumes the staged flags without producing a READY picker, so later READY publication reads latest settings. Non-membership weight/connectivity publications read current flags and do not prematurely consume a pending membership snapshot. This models the pre-latestConfig callback phase at the native publication boundary; full synchronous JS callback sequencing/coalesced observer equivalence remains unverified.

## Numeric grpc-status compatibility at native transport boundary (2026-10-19)

Continues6393e217. Reviewed pinned subchannel-call.ts handleTrailers Number(metadataMap[grpc-status]) and load-balancing-call.ts outputStatus listener/onCallEnded ordering. The native pool previously counted only literal grpc-status text0 as outlier success while Tonic also parsed raw status bytes directly. New grpc_lb_status.rs normalizes first-header values whose numeric conversion is a finite integer0..16 into canonical status text, before both outlier observer and Tonic see initial/trailers-only headers or final body trailers. Application and outlier now share the same supported status conversion boundary; signed/zero-padded/decimal/exponent values, unsigned0x/0o/0b literals, empty/ECMAScript whitespace-only values, negative zero and leading-zero integer strings can canonicalize. UTF8/grammar failures, fractions, nonfinite and out-of-range values remain untouched for existing Tonic handling, rather than inventing an enum code. No claim that this represents arbitrary JavaScript NaN/Infinity/fractional/out-of-enum status codes.

Status normalization changes only reserved grpc-status in the selected response; Entry/body retention, ORCA metrics fields and first-terminal counter guard remain. Missing status and transport/cancellation failures still count as failure on observer Drop. This does not establish complete logical/transport terminal accounting, HTTP fallback, decoder/cardinality/cancellation race parity, nor coverage for Buffered proxy/socket routes that bypass the pool.

Official docs consulted before implementation: gRPC HTTP2 response/trailer/status grammar https://github.com/grpc/grpc/blob/master/doc/PROTOCOL-HTTP2.md and ECMAScript StringToNumber/StringNumericLiteral https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/abstract-operations.html#sec-tonumber-applied-to-the-string-type. The gRPC wire grammar is digit-only; broader Number syntax here preserves the pinned grpc-js1.14.4 receive behavior, not a request to generate noncanonical server statuses. Existing Tonic status boundary and pinned local source retained; Postman api-engineer workflow reused, no workspace/provider operation.

## Outlier local-calendar ejection return time (2026-10-19)

Continues22e0eec7. Pinned grpc-js1.14.4 outlier runChecks constructs returnTime from the ejection timestamp then uses Date.setMilliseconds(getMilliseconds()+duration), not elapsed UTC addition. Exposed add_milliseconds through the existing service deadline calendar module and reused its setter/local fold-gap disambiguation/TimeClip/truncation logic for outlier expiry. ## Process-wide native DNS subchannel registry (2026-10-19)

Continuesc371e0af. SubchannelCache factories now enter a process-wide registry keyed by raw channel target, immutable Endpoint origin and effective connect timeout. Plaintext factories with matching scope share cached singleton owners across logical DNS routes. Existing grpc_transport::channel fixes user agent/other Endpoint settings; any new varying factory setting must be added to scope. RPC metadata/deadline and policy-local counters/weights are not copied into the physical connection scope. Buffered proxy/numeric/socket topology remains separate; this is not full cross-route/global-channel parity. Exact raw-target versus pinned canonical URI alias equality remains a fidelity gate.

Pinned subchannel-pool.ts compares target/address/channelOptions and ChannelCredentials._equals. channel-credentials.ts createSsl builds a new secureContext and _equals compares context/checkServerIdentity by reference, not certificate bytes. Current native channel() likewise constructs a fresh TLS config per invocation, so TLS registry scopes receive a fresh opaque UUID; cloning a cache retains that context scope, and new contexts/CA/client identities/verifiers cannot alias by PEM similarity. No credentials/material hashes are placed in registry keys or logs.

Registry holds cache owners through the existing10s unused-subchannel check rather than dropping them with the last logical handle. Empty owners are pruned only when registry plus the current cleanup/drop reference remain, checked under registry lock; concurrent factory lookup clones under that lock so an owner cannot disappear between lookup and get_or_create. Nested lock order is registry then cache, with cleanup releasing cache lock before pruning. Cleanup still captures Weak owner, stops on empty cache and restarts on use; empty never-used factories prune on Drop. Terminated singleton drivers are excluded/replaced on lookup, and a terminated cleanup task is restarted on use. Runtime-shutdown purge/concurrent-runtime behavior is not established.

## Initial DNS failure logical ownership (2026-10-19)

Initial DNS failure now returns an owned Dynamic route with an Unavailable picker rather than discarding the logical channel. Its resolver worker retries using the existing cached backoff until a successful lookup, with target-retirement cancellation and Owner drop aborts. The policy worker waits for accepted nonempty addresses and creates the desired child policy even when its signature matches the initial default. Successful initial lookups do not enter the bootstrap retry loop. Source contract: pinned grpc-js1.14.4 resolver-dns.ts and resolving-load-balancer.ts; https://github.com/grpc/grpc-node/tree/%40grpc%2Fgrpc-js%401.14.4/packages/grpc-js . Existing Postman api-engineer contract workflow reused.

## DNS recovery Connecting publication (2026-10-19)

Follow-up to3178bfe2: after the first accepted DNS address publication, a route still holding the bootstrap Unavailable picker now publishes its new child immediately, including Connecting. It no longer serves the obsolete initial DNS error while waiting for the child decision. This child becomes current directly, with active signature/config/address revisions adopted, so normal pending-policy promotion cannot retire the same pool later. Publication remains guarded by generation/revision/policy acceptance. Existing routes with a previous child retain their pending replacement behavior. Contract reviewed pinned grpc-js1.14.4 resolving-load-balancer.ts resolverListener/updateState; https://github.com/grpc/grpc-node/blob/%40grpc%2Fgrpc-js%401.14.4/packages/grpc-js/src/resolving-load-balancer.ts .

## Failed re-resolution retry continuation (2026-10-19)

The owned resolution worker now keeps a requested lookup active after DNS failure, waiting for the updated backoff deadline and retrying until success or generation/owner retirement. Previously it consumed the request after one failed lookup and waited for another child notification, potentially stranding a disconnected route with no new connectivity transition. Successful resolution still coalesces requests and adopts the latest child cursor. Existing last accepted addresses remain intact. Contract: pinned grpc-js1.14.4 resolver-dns.ts handleHealthStatus/continueResolving and backoff callback; https://github.com/grpc/grpc-node/blob/%40grpc%2Fgrpc-js%401.14.4/packages/grpc-js/src/resolver-dns.ts .

## Accepted config with fresh DNS addresses (2026-10-19)

Snapshot address acceptance now shares one publication method for address and TXT callbacks. If a newly selected TXT config is invalid but a selector was previously accepted, new DNS addresses are published under that accepted config instead of freezing the old address list. The invalid selected result is preserved, and an initial invalid config without any accepted selector still cannot publish a route. Throttle publication and policy/address revision updates use the working accepted config. This follows pinned grpc-js1.14.4 resolving-load-balancer.ts handleResolverResult previousServiceConfig fallback and child updateAddressList. Contract: https://github.com/grpc/grpc-node/blob/%40grpc%2Fgrpc-js%401.14.4/packages/grpc-js/src/resolving-load-balancer.ts .
